    “We’ve got bigger things to figure out,” Carol continues. “Like—are we doing on-demand feeding? Or a schedule? And the whole cry-it-out thing—”

    “Absolutely not,” Zosia cuts in immediately.

    Carol huffs a small laugh. “Okay, good. Because that sounds like torture.”

    Zosia’s gaze drops back to Ava, her expression softening completely now. She adjusts her hold slightly, her arms wrapping more securely around her.

    “I never want her to feel alone,” she says quietly. “Or unwanted. Unloved.”

    Carol shifts closer, tucking herself into Zosia’s side. Her hand returns to Ava’s head, fingers moving gently through the soft curls.

    “That’s never going to happen,” she says, her voice low but certain. “We won’t let it.”

    Zosia turns her head, pressing a slow, lingering kiss to Carol’s lips.

    “Thank you,” she murmurs when she pulls back. “I love you.”

    “I love you too.” Carol’s gaze drops to the baby between them, something steady settling in her chest. “And her. It’s crazy. I’ve never felt anything like it. From the moment I held her in the hospital, I just—I would die for her. Fucking kill for her. Is that crazy?”

    “Not at all.” Zosia smiles. She leans in, pressing her forehead lightly to Carol’s temple, their daughter still cradled in her hands between them.

    “It’s the three of us now,” she murmurs.

    There’s a quiet certainty in her voice. Something unshakable that quiets some of the doubts in the back of Carol’s mind. Quiets the nagging voice, which sounds a lot like Carol’s mother, telling her she’s going to mess this all up.

    “Us against the world,” Carol agrees with a hum.

    Pulling back so she can look at Zosia properly, her expression shifts, growing almost uncertain. “Um, I’ve actually been thinking about that.”

    “Hmm?” Zosia’s attention shifts from Carol to the baby as Ava grows fussy, seemingly done with feeding. Zosia cradles her with one arm and adjusts her shirt with her free hand.

    Carol waits to have her full attention back before she continues. This is important. “Yeah, so—I was thinking. About us, and Ava, and—”

    Zosia’s brow creases, fearing the worst. “Carol, I—”

    “No, no, it’s okay!” Carol reassures her the moment she sees the unease cross the other woman’s face. “It’s good thinking—at least, I hope it is.”

    Carol takes a deep breath as she props herself up on her elbow. She’s been thinking about this for a while now, not quite sure how to broach the subject.

    She bites her bottom lip, silently praying she doesn’t fuck this up. “I—I want to be with you and Ava. Like, all the time,” she starts, watching Zosia’s expression closely, gauging her reaction to the admission. Zosia smiles fondly at her, still unsure where this is going.

    “And, with the settlement coming through, I was thinking…” Carol takes a deep, steadying breath. She’s stood in front of hundreds of people, bearing her soul through her literature, and it didn’t feel half as terrifying as this.

    “I want us to move in together,” she finally admits, the words rushing out of her mouth. “I know this is your home, but it’s kind of small. We could buy somewhere bigger, with an office for me and a nice fancy kitchen for you… and a big yard for Ava to play in. What do you think?”

    “I think…” Zosia begins, a smile playing on her lips that eases some of Carol’s fears, “that sounds a lot like your place.”

    Carol flushes. “Yeah, but it’s not like I’d expect you to move in there. I mean, it would hardly be fair—”

    Zosia presses a hand to Carol’s chest, Ava still cradled between them on the pillow. “I like your place,” she says, cutting off Carol’s rambling.

    Carol ducks her head. “Are you sure it wouldn’t be too…” She trails off with a wave of her hand, unsure how to put what she feels into words. She’s not sure she could move into Zosia’s place, where the ghost of Malik lingers in every corner, in every piece of furniture, and even the paint on the walls. “I mean, we could start fresh? Find somewhere new. Somewhere that’s just ours.”

    Zosia’s eyes soften, understanding finally dawning as she shakes her head. “Two of us moving, and with the baby, it’s a lot. Besides, I like your place. And Manousos is right next door.”

    “Built-in babysitter,” Carol agrees with a grin. “And I’d give it six months until Koumba moves himself in.”

    Beside her, Zosia chuckles in agreement. “He seems fond of Manousos. I think he’d be good for him. It’s time he settled down.”

    Carol chews the inside of her lip, only half paying attention. Her thoughts are on her house, how easy it would be to turn the guest room into Ava’s nursery.

    “Hey.” Zosia reaches out, cradling Carol’s cheek. “Where’d you go?”

    “Sorry,” Carol apologises. She turns her head, pressing her lips to Zosia’s palm. “Are you sure about this? About moving in with me?”

    Zosia doesn’t hesitate to nod. “I would love to. We would love to.”

    The words settle warmly in Carol’s chest, driving out the last remnants of something that’s lived there for a very long time. Fear. Grief. Loneliness. She isn’t entirely sure.

    Maybe all three.

    Beside them, Ava lets out a tiny sound against Zosia’s chest, her mouth falling open as she drifts deeper into sleep, one impossibly small hand curled against Zosia’s finger. Carol’s gaze drops to her automatically, her heart swelling in a way she doesn’t think she’ll ever get used to. She shifts closer, one arm sliding carefully around Zosia’s shoulders while her other hand comes to rest gently over Ava’s back, covering almost all of it. She’s still so impossibly small.

    The late afternoon light spills through the bedroom window, washing over the bed in soft golden stripes. It catches in Ava’s dark curls, glows against Zosia’s pale skin, turns everything warm. Safe.

    Zosia in her arms.

    Ava between them.

    The quiet rhythm of their breathing slowly falling into sync.

    Carol thinks, with sudden fierce certainty, that whatever comes next—

    They’ll face it together.

    As a family.
